with probability at least
1-e^{-t},

\displaystyle\left|\langle\bar{T}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}T(X),u\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma_{\rho}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\bigvee\frac{2}{\rho}\frac{t}{n},

which also implies that, for all
p\geq 1,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\left\|\langle\bar{T}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}T(X),u\rangle\right\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim\sigma_{\rho}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\bigvee\frac{1}{\rho}\frac{p}{n}.

(8.2)

The next statement follows from Proposition
7.1
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Suppose Assumption
7.3
on the norm of Banach space
E
holds.
Also assume that for some
\rho>0,

B(\theta,\rho)\subset{\rm Int}\Theta.

Then, for all
p\geq 1

\displaystyle\left\|\|\bar{T}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}T(X)\|\right\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim C\sigma_{\rho}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\frac{C}{\rho}\frac{d}{n}\vee C\sigma_{\rho}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{C}{\rho}\frac{p}{n}.

(8.3)

Finally, note that, if
\Theta=E^{\ast},
we can take
\rho=\infty
and set
\sigma_{\infty}^{2}:=\sup_{\theta\in E^{\ast}}\|\Sigma_{\theta}\|.

In this case, bounds (
8.2
) and (
8.3
) become subgaussian:

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\left\|\langle\bar{T}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}T(X),u\rangle\right\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim\sigma_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}

(8.4)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\|\bar{T}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}T(X)\|\right\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim C\sigma_{\infty}\Bigl(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\Bigr).

(8.5)

Example 8.2
.

An important example of exponential model with matrix parameter is

\displaystyle P_{\theta}(dx)=\frac{1}{Z(\theta)}\exp\{\langle x\otimes x,\theta\rangle\}\mu(dx),x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

where the parameter
\theta
is a symmetric
d\times d
matrix and
\mu
is a Borel probability measure in

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}.
The statistic generating this exponential family is
T(x)=x\otimes x.
It also takes values in the
space of symmetric matrices. This space will be equipped
with the operator norm and the resulting Banach space will be denoted by
E.
Its dual space
E^{\ast}
is again the linear space of
symmetric
d\times d
matrices equipped with the nuclear norm. With these conventions, statistic
T(x)=x\otimes x
takes values in
E

and the parameter space
\Theta=\{\theta:Z(\theta)<+\infty\}
is a convex subset of
E^{\ast}.
For Assumption
8.1
to be satisfied, it would be enough
to assume that the functions
1,x_{i}x_{j}:1\leq i\leq j\leq d
are linearly independent on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and that

{\rm l.s.}(\Theta)=E^{\ast}.

Note that for our exponential model

\Psi(\theta)={\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(X\otimes X),X\sim P_{\theta},\theta\in\Theta.

In addition to mean zero normal model (with non-standard parametrization), this class of exponential families
includes Bingham distribution for directional data (with
\mu
being the uniform distribution on the unit sphere
S^{d-1}
)
and Ising model (with
\mu
being the uniform distribution on the binary cube
\{-1,1\}^{d}
).
For these two models, the support of measure
\mu
is bounded, which implies that
\Theta=E^{\ast}
(this, of course, could be the case even if the support of measure
\mu
is not bounded).
The condition
\Theta=E^{\ast}
also implies that
\Theta_{1}=E^{\ast}
and, as a consequence of the results by Chencov discussed
in Section
8.1
,
{\rm Int}\ {\rm csupp}(\mu\circ T^{-1})=\Psi(\Theta_{1}).

Let

\bar{T}_{n}:=n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{j}\otimes X_{j}.

Since
E^{\ast}
is the space of symmetric matrices equipped with the nuclear norm, using the spectral decomposition of matrix
U\in E^{\ast},
it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\sup_{U\in E^{\ast},\|U\|\leq 1}\Bigl\|\langle\bar{T}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X),U\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}=\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|\leq 1}\Bigl\|\langle(\bar{T}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X))y,y\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}.

For
\Sigma_{\theta}:={\rm cov}_{\theta}(T(X)),
we have

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{\theta}\|=\sup_{U\in E^{\ast},\|U\|\leq 1}\langle\Sigma_{\theta}U,U\rangle=\sup_{y,y^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|,\|y^{\prime}\|\leq 1}\langle\Sigma_{\theta}(y\otimes y),(y^{\prime}\otimes y^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}\langle X\otimes X,y\otimes y\rangle^{2}=\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}\langle X,y\rangle^{4}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)\leq\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in B(\theta,r)}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta^{\prime}}\langle X,y\rangle^{4}:=\bar{\sigma}_{r}^{2}(\theta)

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\infty}^{2}\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|y\|\leq 1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}\langle X,y\rangle^{4}:=\bar{\sigma}_{\infty}^{2}.

If
B(\theta,r)\subset{\rm Int}\Theta,
then the following Bernstein type bound holds for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
\|y\|\leq 1
and all
p\geq 1:

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle(\bar{T}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X))y,y\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}\lesssim\bar{\sigma}_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n}.

Moreover, if
{\mathcal{N}}
is a
1/4
-net for
S^{d-1}
of cardinality
\leq 9^{d},
then

\displaystyle\|A\|\leq 2\max_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}}|\langle Ay,y\rangle|,A\in E.

Thus, the bound of Proposition
8.2
with
{\mathcal{M}}:=\{y\otimes y:y\in{\mathcal{N}}\}
implies that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\|\bar{T}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X)\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}\lesssim\bar{\sigma}_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{d}{n}\vee\bar{\sigma}_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n}.

8.3
Bounds on the
L_{p}
-errors in functional estimation

Since, for regular exponential families,
\Psi
is a
C^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism between
{\rm Int}\Theta
and its image
\Psi({\rm Int}\Theta)

(which coincides with
{\rm Int}\ {\rm csupp}(\mu\circ T^{-1})
) and, for natural parameter
t=\Psi(\theta)
there exists a simple estimator
\bar{T}_{n},

it is reasonable to reduce the problem of estimation of functional
f(\theta)
based on i.i.d. observations
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\sim P_{\theta}

to the problem of estimation of functional
(f\circ\Psi^{-1})(t)
of natural parameter
t.
With such an approach, the smoothness conditions
will be imposed on
f\circ\Psi^{-1},
which, under proper smoothness of map
\Psi^{-1},
could be reduced to the smoothness of
f.

We use the sample split described in Section
2
to define estimators
\bar{T}_{n^{(0)}}
and
\bar{T}_{n^{(k)}_{j}},j=1,\dots,k,k=1,\dots,m

and, based on these estimators, to construct estimator
T_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
(see (
2.3
)) and its truncated version

\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1},M}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
(see (
2.4
)).

The next result is a corollary of Theorem
7.1
. Of course, to apply this theorem, one has to consider the identifiable model

{\mathcal{P}}:=\{P_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}
and set
\theta(P):=\theta
for
P=P_{\theta}\in{\mathcal{P}}.
Denote

\Sigma_{\theta}:={\rm cov}_{\theta}(T(X))=\Psi^{\prime}(\theta),\theta\in{\rm Int}\Theta
and

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta):=\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in B(\theta,r)\cap{\rm Int}\Theta}\|\Sigma_{\theta^{\prime}}\|,r>0.

For
r=\infty,

\sigma_{\infty}^{2}:=\sup_{\theta\in{\rm Int}\Theta}\|\Sigma_{\theta}\|.

Theorem 8.1
.

Suppose the norm of space
E
satisfies Assumption
7.3
. Let
f:E^{\ast}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
be a uniformly bounded functional.
Suppose that
f\circ\Psi^{-1}
is
m
times continuously differentiable in a neighborhood
U=B(t,\delta)
for some
t\in\Psi({\rm Int}\Theta)
and
\delta\in(0,1],

and, moreover,

(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}
satisfies the Hölder condition with exponent
\rho\in(0,1]
in
U.

Let
\theta:=\Psi^{-1}(t)
and suppose that, for some
r>0,

B(\theta;r)\subset{\rm Int}\Theta.

(i) Assume that
n^{(0)}\asymp_{m}n
and
n_{j}^{(k)}\asymp_{m}n
,
j=1,\cdots,k
,
k=1,\cdots,m.

Moreover, assume that for a sufficiently large numerical constant
C^{\prime}>0,

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\left(C\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\frac{C}{r}\frac{d}{n}\right)\leq\delta.

Then, with some constant
c_{1}>0
depending on
m,C,
for
M\geq\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}
and
p\geq 1,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,C}\max_{0\leq k\leq m}\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(k)}\|_{L_{\infty}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n}\right)

\displaystyle+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}\|_{{\rm Lip}_{\rho}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\frac{1}{r^{s}}\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{p}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\frac{1}{r^{s}}\Bigl(\frac{p}{n}\Bigr)^{s}\right)+

\displaystyle+(\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+M)\exp\Bigl\{-c_{1}\frac{n}{p}\Bigl(\frac{\delta^{2}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)}\wedge\delta r\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

(ii) Assume that, for all
j=1,\dots,k,k=1,\dots,m,

n_{j}^{(k)}\asymp_{m}n.

If
M\geq\|f\|_{\infty}+\|f\|_{{\rm Lip}}\delta,
then

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)-\langle\bar{T}_{1}^{(1)}-\Psi(\theta),(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\rangle\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,C}\max_{2\leq k\leq m}\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(k)}\|_{L_{\infty}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n}\right)\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}\vee\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}\right)

\displaystyle+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}\|_{{\rm Lip}_{\rho}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\frac{1}{r^{s}}\Bigl(\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\frac{1}{r^{s}}\Bigl(\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+M+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\|\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{d}{n}\vee\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{1}{r}\frac{p}{n}\right)\right)\exp\Bigl\{-c_{1}\frac{n^{(0)}}{p}\Bigl(\frac{\delta^{2}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)}\wedge\delta r\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

Remark 8.1
.

Note that, if
\Theta=E^{\ast},
we can set
r:=\infty.
Then,
under the assumption that, for a sufficiently large constant
C^{\prime}>0,

C^{\prime}C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq\delta,

the following bound holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}\lesssim_{s,C}\max_{0\leq k\leq m}\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(k)}\|_{L_{\infty}(U)}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}

\displaystyle+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}\|_{{\rm Lip}_{\rho}(U)}\sigma_{\infty}^{s}\Bigl(\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\Bigl(\frac{p}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\Bigr)+(\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+M)\exp\Bigl\{-c_{1}\frac{n}{p}\frac{\delta^{2}}{\sigma_{\infty}^{2}}\Bigr\}.

Remark 8.2
.

Under the assumptions that, for large enough constant
C^{\prime}>0,

\displaystyle r\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\ {\rm and}\ C^{\prime}C\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq\delta\leq 1,

the first bound of the theorem takes the following form:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,C}\max_{0\leq k\leq m}\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(k)}\|_{L_{\infty}(U)}\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}\|_{{\rm Lip}_{\rho}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{p}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)^{s}\right)+

\displaystyle+(\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+M)\exp\Bigl\{-c_{1}\frac{\sqrt{nd}}{p}\frac{\delta}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\Bigr\}.

Moreover, if for large enough constant
C^{\prime}>0,

\displaystyle r\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\ {\rm and}\ C^{\prime}C\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\Bigl(1\vee\frac{\log\frac{n}{\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)d}}{d}\Bigr)\leq\delta\leq 1,

then, for
M=\|f\|_{L_{\infty}},

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}\lesssim_{s,C}\|f\circ\Psi^{-1}\|_{C^{s}(U)}\Bigl[\frac{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s/2}\Bigr].

(8.6)

Remark 8.3
.

If

\displaystyle r\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\ {\rm and}\ C^{\prime}C\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\leq\delta\leq 1,

then the second bound of the theorem takes the form

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)-\langle\bar{T}_{1}^{(1)}-\Psi(\theta),(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\rangle\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,C}\max_{2\leq k\leq m}\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(k)}\|_{L_{\infty}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)\right)\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\vee\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)\right)

\displaystyle+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{(m)}\|_{{\rm Lip}_{\rho}(U)}\left(\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s/2}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{p}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s/2}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)^{s}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+M+\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\|\left(\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\Bigl(1\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{d}}\Bigr)\right)\right)\exp\Bigl\{-c_{1}\frac{\sqrt{n^{(0)}d}}{p}\frac{\delta}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\Bigr\}.

Moreover, if for large enough constant
C^{\prime}>0,

\displaystyle r\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{r}(\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\ {\rm and}\ C^{\prime}C\sigma_{r}(\theta)\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\Bigl(1\vee\frac{\log\frac{n^{(0)}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)d}}{d}\Bigr)\leq\delta\leq 1,

then, for
M=\|f\|_{L_{\infty}}+\|f\|_{\rm Lip},

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-f(\theta)-\langle\bar{T}_{1}^{(1)}-\Psi(\theta),(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\rangle\right\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,C}\|f\circ\Psi^{-1}\|_{C^{s}(U)}\Bigl[\sigma_{r}^{2}(\theta)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}}\vee\sigma_{r}^{s}(\theta)\Bigl(\frac{d}{n^{(0)}}\Bigr)^{s/2}\Bigr].

Remark 8.4
.

It is easy to see that the quantity
\sigma_{r}(\theta)
in the statement of Theorem
8.1
and in remarks
8.1
,
8.2

and
8.3
could be replaced by an arbitrary upper bound.

Note that Theorem
5.1
of Section
5
easily follows from bound (
8.6
).

Finally, we provide the proof of Corollary
5.2
.

proof.

If
n^{(0)}\asymp\frac{n}{\log n},
we would have
n_{1}^{(1)}=(1+o(1))n.

Using the bounds on the rates of convergence in CLT in Wasserstein distance
[
34
]
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in V}\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{2,P_{\theta}}\Bigl(\frac{\langle n^{1/2}(\bar{T}_{n}-\Psi(\theta)),u\rangle}{\langle\Sigma_{\theta}u,u\rangle^{1/2}},Z\Bigr)\lesssim\sup_{\theta\in V}\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\frac{\|\langle T(X)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X),u\rangle\|_{L_{4}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}^{2}}{\|\langle T(X)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}T(X),u\rangle\|_{L_{2}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{\sqrt{\kappa(V)}}{\sqrt{n}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in V}\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{2,P_{\theta}}\Bigl(\langle n^{1/2}(\bar{T}_{n}-\Psi(\theta)),u\rangle,\langle\Sigma_{\theta}u,u\rangle^{1/2}Z\Bigr)\lesssim\sigma_{\max}(W)\frac{\sqrt{\kappa(V)}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Therefore, it easily follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(G)}\leq 1}\sup_{\theta\in V}W_{2,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}\Bigl\langle\bar{T}_{1}^{(1)}-\Psi(\theta),(f\circ\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)\Bigr\rangle,\sigma_{f,\Psi}(\Psi(\theta))Z\Bigr)\to 0\ {\rm as}\ n\to\infty.

Together with the bound of part (ii) of Theorem
5.1
, this implies,
under the assumptions that
d\leq n^{\alpha}
and
s>\frac{1}{1-\alpha},
the asymptotic normality (
5.2
)
of estimator
\tilde{T}_{f\circ\Psi^{-1}}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})

as well as the limit of its
L_{2}
-error (
5.3
).

∎

9
Proofs of the upper bounds for high-dimensional models with independent low-dimensional components

We will provide below the proofs of the results of Section
3
.
We start with the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

proof.

The proof is based on the following proposition that provides a way to check Assumption
2.1
for estimator
\hat{\theta}_{n}
in terms of similar
assumptions on its components
\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)},j=1,\dots,n.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
p\geq 2.
Then

(a)

For all
P\in{\mathcal{P}},

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1,u\in E^{\ast}}\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P),u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{p}{\log p}\Bigl(\sqrt{\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{p,j}(P_{j})}{n}}+\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}b_{j}(P_{j})}{n}\Bigr)+\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}b_{j}(P)}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}},

where
C>0
is a numerical constant.

(b)

For all
P\in P,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P)\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\leq\sqrt{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}d_{p,j}(P_{j})}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}.

proof.

First note that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P),u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\leq\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n},u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}+\Bigl\|\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P),u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}.

(9.1)

We have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n},u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}=\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}.

Denote

\zeta_{j}:=\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle,j=1,\dots,d.

Since
\zeta_{j},j=1,\dots,d
are independent mean zero r.v., we can use the bound of Th. 1.5.11 in
[
7
]
to get that, for all
p\geq 2,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n},u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\zeta_{j}\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\biggl(\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}\zeta_{j}^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\bigvee\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}|\zeta_{j}|^{p}\biggr)^{1/p}\biggr).

Note that, for
p\geq 2,

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{j}\|_{L_{2}}\leq\|\zeta_{j}\|_{L_{p}}\leq\gamma_{n,p}(P)\|u^{(j)}\|,j=1,\dots,d,

where

\displaystyle\gamma_{n,p}(P):=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{\|u_{j}\|\leq 1,u_{j}\in E_{j}^{\ast}}\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)},u_{j}\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})},P\in{\mathcal{P}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n},u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\gamma_{n,p}(P)\biggl(\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\bigvee\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{p}\biggr)^{1/p}\biggr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\gamma_{n,p}(P)\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{\log p}\gamma_{n,p}(P)\|u\|.

(9.2)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P),u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}=\Bigl|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j}),u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl\|{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\Bigr\|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\beta_{n}(P)\|u\|,

(9.3)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{n}(P):=\|{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P)\|=\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl\|{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\Bigr\|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2},P\in{\mathcal{P}}.

It immediately follows from bounds (
9.1
), (
9
)
and (
9
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1,u\in E^{\ast}}\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P),u\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\leq C\frac{p}{\log p}\gamma_{n,p}(P)+\beta_{n}(P)

with some numerical constant
C>0.

Also note that, for
p\geq 2,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P)\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}=\Bigl\|\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\|^{2}\Bigr\|_{L_{p/2}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}^{1/2}\leq\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\|^{2}\Bigr\|_{L_{p/2}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}(P_{j})\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\tau_{n,p}(P)\sqrt{d},

where

\displaystyle\tau_{n,p}(P):=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta_{j}(P_{j})\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})},P\in{\mathcal{P}}.

To complete the proof, it remains to further bound the quantities
\beta_{n}(P),\gamma_{n,p}(P)
and
\tau_{n,p}(P)

under the conditions of the proposition.
We easily get that

\displaystyle\beta_{n}(P)=\|{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta(P)\|\leq\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}b_{j}(P_{j})}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}.

Also,

\displaystyle\gamma_{n,p}(P)\leq\sqrt{\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{p,j}(P_{j})}{n}}+\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}b_{j}(P_{j})}{n}.

and

\displaystyle\tau_{n,p}(P)\leq\sqrt{\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}d_{p,j}(P_{j})}{n}}.

This implies the bounds of the proposition.

∎

Note that, if
d\lesssim n
and

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq d}b_{j}(P_{j})\lesssim 1,\ \max_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{p,j}(P_{j})\lesssim 1,\ \max_{1\leq j\leq d}d_{ps,j}(P_{j})\lesssim 1,

then, for estimator
\hat{\theta}_{n},
we have
a_{p}(P)\lesssim_{p}1
and
d_{ps}(P)\lesssim_{ps}d.

The claims of the theorem now follow from the bounds of Theorem
2.1
.

∎

Next we provide a proof of Corollary
3.1
.

proof.

First note that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta_{j}}\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}\Bigr\|\leq\frac{W_{1,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}),\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})\Bigr)}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})}{n}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u^{(j)}\|\leq 1}\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sup_{\|u^{(j)}\|\leq 1}\Bigl\|\langle\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)}),u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}),\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})\Bigr)}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sup_{\|u^{(j)}\|\leq 1}\|\langle Z,I_{j}(\theta^{(j)})^{-1/2}u^{(j)}\rangle\|_{L_{p}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\frac{\|I_{j}(\theta^{(j)})^{-1/2}\|}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})}{n}

and, similarly,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\|\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})}\lesssim_{p}\|I_{j}(\theta^{(j)})^{-1/2}\|\sqrt{\frac{l_{j}}{n}}+\frac{C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})}{n}.

The above bounds allow one to check condition (
3.1
) of Theorem
3.1
, which yields the claims of the corollary.

∎

Finally, we provide a proof of Proposition
3.2
.

proof.

We need to check that Assumption
2.2
holds for estimator

\hat{\theta}_{n}=(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(1)},\dots,\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(d)}).
To this end, we obtain a bound on the accuracy of approximation
of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta)
by
\xi(\theta):=(\xi_{1}(\theta^{(1)}),\dots,\xi_{d}(\theta^{(d)}))

in the
W_{p}
-distance.

Lemma 9.1
.

For all
p\geq 2,
the following bound holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta,u\rangle,\langle\xi(\theta),u\rangle\Bigr)\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}.

Thus, under the assumption
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{\theta^{(j)}\in\Theta_{j}}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\lesssim 1
and
d=d_{n}=o(n)
as
n\to\infty,

we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta,u\rangle,\langle\xi(\theta),u\rangle\Bigr)\to 0.

proof.

Let
(\eta^{(1)},\zeta^{(1)}),\dots,(\eta^{(d)},\zeta^{(d)})
be independent r.v. such that
\eta^{(j)}\overset{d}{=}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)})

and
\zeta^{(j)}\overset{d}{=}\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})
for all
j=1,\dots,d.
Denote
\eta:=(\eta^{(1)},\dots,\eta^{(d)})
and
\zeta:=(\zeta^{(1)},\dots,\zeta^{(d)}).

We have

\displaystyle W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta,u\rangle,\langle\xi(\theta),u\rangle\Bigr)\leq\|\langle\eta-\zeta,u\rangle\|_{L_{p}}=\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbb{E}}(\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}),u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr|+\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle-{\mathbb{E}}\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle)\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}.

The first term in the right hand side could be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\Bigl|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbb{E}}(\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}),u^{(j)}\rangle\Bigr|\leq\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|{\mathbb{E}}\eta^{(j)}-{\mathbb{E}}\zeta^{(j)}\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|u\|\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}^{2}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}),\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})\Bigr)\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\|u\|\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}.

For the second term, we argue as in the proof of Proposition
9.1
to get, for
p\geq 2,
the bound

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle-{\mathbb{E}}\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle)\Bigr\|_{L_{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\Bigl(\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}|\langle\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)},u^{(j)}\rangle|^{p}\Bigr)^{1/p}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\Bigl(\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{2}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{p}\|u^{(j)}\|^{p}\Bigr)^{1/p}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\Bigl(\max_{1\leq j\leq d}{\mathbb{E}}^{1/2}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{2}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\max_{1\leq j\leq d}{\mathbb{E}}^{1/p}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{p}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|u^{(j)}\|^{p}\Bigr)^{1/p}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\|u\|\max_{1\leq j\leq d}{\mathbb{E}}^{1/p}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{p}.

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta,u\rangle,\langle\xi(\theta),u\rangle\Bigr)\lesssim\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{p}{\log p}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}{\mathbb{E}}^{1/p}\|\eta^{(j)}-\zeta^{(j)}\|^{p}.

Minimizing the right hand side over all
\eta^{(j)}\overset{d}{=}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)})

and
\zeta^{(j)}\overset{d}{=}\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})
yields the bound

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\langle\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta,u\rangle,\langle\xi(\theta),u\rangle\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{p}{\log p}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}W_{p,{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta^{(j)}),\xi^{(j)}(\theta^{(j)})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{p}{\log p}\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim\frac{p}{\log p}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}C_{p,j}(\theta^{(j)})\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}.

∎

Applying this lemma for
u=f^{\prime}(\theta)
and observing that
{\mathbb{E}}\langle\xi(\theta),f^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle^{2}=\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta),

it is easy to deduce the claim of Proposition
3.2
from Corollary
3.1
(ii).

∎

10
Proofs of upper bounds for functionals of covariance

We provide a proof of Theorem
4.1
of Section
4
.

proof.
We will use the following simple fact (probably, well known, but we will sketch its proof for completeness).

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
X
be a centered subgaussian and pre-gaussian r.v. in
E
with covariance operator
\Sigma
and let
\{x_{n}\}
be
the sequence of vectors from representation of
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma)
in Proposition
4.1
. Denote by
{\mathbb{H}}_{X}

the closure of the set
\{\langle X,u\rangle:u\in E^{\ast}\}
in the space
L_{2}({\mathbb{P}})
of real valued random variables. Then, there exists
an orthonormal sequence
\{\xi_{n}\}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{X}\subset L_{2}({\mathbb{P}})
of r.v. such that, for all
u\in E^{\ast},

\displaystyle\langle X,u\rangle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\xi_{n}\langle x_{n},u\rangle\ {\rm in}\ L_{2}({\mathbb{P}})

and, moreover,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl\|X-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\xi_{n}x_{n}\Bigr\|\to 0\ {\rm as}\ N\to\infty.

proof.

It is easy to check that
{\mathbb{H}}_{X}
is a linear subspace and all r.v. in
{\mathbb{H}}_{X}
are centered and subgaussian:
for all
\eta\in{\mathbb{H}}_{X},

{\mathbb{E}}\eta=0

and

\displaystyle\|\eta\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\|\eta\|_{L_{2}}.

For
u\in E^{\ast},
define
\bar{u}:=(\langle x_{n},u\rangle:n\geq 1)\in\ell_{2}.
Since vectors
\{x_{n}:n\geq 1\}
are linearly independent,
it is easy to check that the closure of the set
\{\bar{u}:u\in E^{\ast}\}
coincides with
\ell_{2}.
Recall that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\langle X,u\rangle\langle X,v\rangle=\langle\Sigma u,v\rangle=\sum_{n\geq 1}\langle x_{n},u\rangle\langle x_{n},v\rangle=\langle\bar{u},\bar{v}\rangle_{\ell_{2}},u,v\in E^{\ast}.

Thus, the mapping
\bar{u}\mapsto\langle X,u\rangle
is an isometry between linear spaces
\{\bar{u}:u\in E^{\ast}\}

and
\{\langle X,u\rangle:u\in E^{\ast}\},
and it could be extended by continuity to an isometry
J
between their closures
\ell_{2}
and
{\mathbb{H}}_{X}.

Let
\{e_{n}:n\geq 1\}
be the canonical basis of
\ell_{2}
and denote
\xi_{n}:=Je_{n},n\geq 1.
Then

\{\xi_{n}:n\geq 1\}
is an orthonormal basis of subspace
{\mathbb{H}}_{X}
of
L_{2}({\mathbb{P}}),
and, moreover, for all
u\in E^{\ast},

\displaystyle\langle X,u\rangle=J\bar{u}=\sum_{n\geq 1}\xi_{n}\langle x_{n},u\rangle

with convergence of the series in
L_{2}({\mathbb{P}}).

To conclude the proof, denote
X_{>N}:=X-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\xi_{n}x_{n}
and
Y_{>N}:=Y-\sum_{n=1}^{N}g_{n}x_{n}.

Note that
\langle X_{>N},u\rangle,u\in E^{\ast}
is a centered subgaussian process whereas
\langle Y_{>N},u\rangle,u\in E^{\ast}

is a centered Gaussian process. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\langle X_{>N},u\rangle-\langle X_{>N},v\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\|\langle X_{>N},u\rangle-\langle X_{>N},v\rangle\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle=C\Bigl(\sum_{n>N}\langle x_{n},u-v\rangle^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}=C\|\langle Y_{>N},u\rangle-\langle Y_{>N},v\rangle\|_{L_{2}},\ u,v\in E^{\ast}.

Using Talagrand’s upper generic chaining bound on subgaussian process
\langle X_{>N},u\rangle,u\in E^{\ast},\|u\|\leq 1
along with his lower generic chaining bound on Gaussian process
\langle Y_{>N},u\rangle,u\in E^{\ast},\|u\|\leq 1
yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl\|X-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\xi_{n}x_{n}\Bigr\|={\mathbb{E}}\|X_{>N}\|={\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\langle X_{>N},u\rangle\lesssim C{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\langle Y_{>N},u\rangle=C{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl\|X-\sum_{n=1}^{N}g_{n}x_{n}\Bigr\|.

It easily follows from the claims of Proposition
4.1
and Gaussian concentration inequality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl\|X-\sum_{n=1}^{N}g_{n}x_{n}\Bigr\|\to 0\ {\rm as}\ n\to\infty,

implying the last statement.

∎

To apply Theorem
7.1
, we need to obtain bounds on the
L_{p}
-norms

\sup_{\|U\|\leq 1}\|\langle\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma,U\rangle\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}

and

\|\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma\|\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})},

in the first of these quantities, the supremum being over the unit ball of the dual space of
L(E^{\ast},E).

The following simple proposition holds.

Proposition 10.2
.

Under the above assumptions and notations, for all
p\geq 1,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|U\|\leq 1}\Bigl\|\langle\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma,U\rangle\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\lesssim C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\Bigl(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{p}{n}\Bigr)

(10.1)

and

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma\|\Bigr\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}\lesssim C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\Bigl(\sqrt{\frac{{\bf r}(\Sigma)}{n}}\vee\frac{{\bf r}(\Sigma)}{n}\vee\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\vee\frac{p}{n}\Bigr).

(10.2)

proof.

Since

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma,U\rangle=n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle X_{j}\otimes X_{j},U\rangle-{\mathbb{E}}\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle,

it is enough to show that
\|\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|U\|.

Then, bound (
10.1
) would follow from Bernstein inequality for subexponential r.v.
Moreover, it would be enough to prove the bound on
\|\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}
only in the case
of r.v.
X
taking values in a finite-dimensional subspace
L\subset E.
Indeed, in the general case, one can use approximation
of
X
by
X^{(N)}:=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\xi_{n}x_{n}.
By Proposition
10.1
,
{\mathbb{E}}\|X-X^{(N)}\|\to 0
as
N\to\infty.

Thus, there exists a subsequence
N_{k},k\geq 1
such that
\|X-X^{(N_{k})}\|\to 0
as
k\to\infty
a.s., which also
implies
X^{(N_{k})}\otimes X^{(N_{k})}\to X\otimes X
as
k\to\infty
a.s. in the operator norm. If, for some numerical constant
D>0

and for all
N\geq 1,
we have

\displaystyle\|\langle X^{(N)}\otimes X^{(N)},U\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq DC^{2}\|\Sigma^{(N)}\|\|U\|,

then we also have, for some numerical constant
D^{\prime}>0
and for all
p\geq 1,N\geq 1,

\displaystyle\|\langle X^{(N)}\otimes X^{(N)},U\rangle\|_{L_{p}}\leq D^{\prime}pC^{2}\|\Sigma^{(N)}\|\|U\|,

where
\Sigma^{(N)}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}x_{k}\otimes x_{k}
is the covariance operator of
X^{(N)}.

Since
\|\Sigma^{(N)}\|\leq\|\Sigma\|,
we can write

\displaystyle\|\langle X^{(N)}\otimes X^{(N)},U\rangle\|_{L_{p}}\leq D^{\prime}pC^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|U\|,

and, setting
N=N_{k},
it is easy to justify passing to the limit as
k\to\infty
to get

\displaystyle\|\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle\|_{L_{p}}\leq D^{\prime}pC^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|U\|.

This, clearly, implies the bound
\|\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|U\|.

Thus, we can now consider the case when
X=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\xi_{j}x_{j}
with
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\in E
being linearly independent vectors and

\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N}\in{\mathbb{H}}_{X}
being centered orthonormal r.v.. In this case, we can write

\displaystyle\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\langle x_{i}\otimes x_{j},U\rangle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}a_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j},

where

a_{ij}:=\frac{1}{2}\langle x_{i}\otimes x_{j}+x_{j}\otimes x_{i},U\rangle.

We will now prove the following bound on the nuclear norm of symmetric matrix
A:=(a_{ij})_{i,j=1}^{N}:

\|A\|_{1}\leq\|U\|\|\Sigma\|.
Indeed, by the duality between the operator norm and the nuclear norm,

\|A\|_{1}=\sup\{\langle A,B\rangle:\|B\|\leq 1\}.

We have

\displaystyle\langle A,B\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}a_{ij}b_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl\langle\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}b_{ij}(x_{i}\otimes x_{j}+x_{j}\otimes x_{i}),U\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle=\Bigl\langle\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}b_{ij}x_{i}\otimes x_{j},U\Bigr\rangle\leq\|U\|\Bigl\|\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}b_{ij}x_{i}\otimes x_{j}\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\|U\|\sup_{\|u\|,\|v\|\leq 1}\sum_{i,j}b_{ij}\langle x_{i},u\rangle\langle x_{i},v\rangle\leq\|U\|\|B\|\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\langle x_{j},u\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\|U\|\|B\|\sup_{\|u\|\leq 1}\langle\Sigma u,u\rangle\leq\|U\|\|B\|\|\Sigma\|,

which implies that
\|A\|_{1}\leq\|U\|\|\Sigma\|.

The condition that
X=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{j}x_{j}
is a centered subgaussian random vector implies
that
\xi:=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})
is a centered subgaussian r.v. in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N},
for which we have the bound

\displaystyle\|\langle\xi,t\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\|t\|_{\ell_{2}},t\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}.

Let
\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{N}

be the eigenvalues of symmetric matrix
A
and
\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{d}
be the corresponding orthonormal eigenvectors.
Then

\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}a_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}\langle\xi,\phi_{j}\rangle^{2}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\|\langle X\otimes X,U\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}\langle\xi,\phi_{j}\rangle^{2}\Bigr\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\lambda_{j}|\|\langle\xi,\phi_{j}\rangle^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\lambda_{j}|\|\langle\xi,\phi_{j}\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leq C^{2}\|A\|_{1}\leq C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|U\|.

As a result, we proved bound (
10.1
).

Since r.v.
X
is both subgaussian and pregaussian, the proof of (
10.2
) follows from the Bernstein type bounds on

\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma\|
of Theorem 9 in Koltchinskii and Lounici (2017) (which itself relies on generic chaining bounds obtained
earlier in the papers by Mendelson, Bednorz and Dirksen): for all
t\geq 1
with probability at least
1-e^{-t}

\displaystyle\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma\|\lesssim C^{2}\|\Sigma\|\Bigl(\sqrt{\frac{{\bf r}(\Sigma)}{n}}\vee\frac{{\bf r}(\Sigma)}{n}\vee\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\vee\frac{t}{n}\Bigr).

∎

It follows from Proposition
10.2
that, for
X\sim P
with covariance
\Sigma=\Sigma_{P}
satisfying the above conditions,
Bernstein type bounds on
\sup_{\|U\|\leq 1}\|\langle\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma,U\rangle\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}
and on

\|\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma\|\|_{L_{p}({\mathbb{P}}_{P})}
of Theorem
7.1
hold
with
\sigma(P)=U(P)=\|\Sigma\|
and
d_{1}(P)=\|\Sigma\|^{2}{\bf r}(\Sigma),d_{2}(P)=\|\Sigma\|{\bf r}(\Sigma).
Using Theorem
7.1
, we
can conclude the proof.

∎

11
Lower bounds

In this section, we provide the proofs of minimax lower bounds of sections
2
,
3
and
5
.
We start with the proof of local minimax lower bound of Theorem
2.3
.

proof.

Note that it is enough to prove the claim under the assumptions that
\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\leq 1/2
and

\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}\leq 1.
Otherwise, since
D\geq 2
and
\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\geq 1,

the right hand side of the inequality would be non-positive and the bound would trivially hold.

Let
h:=I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})

and define
\theta_{t}:=\theta_{0}+\frac{t}{\sqrt{n}}h,t\in[-c,c].
We will choose
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|=\delta,

(11.1)

which implies
\theta_{t}\in B(\theta_{0},\delta),t\in[-c,c].

Consider the following one-dimensional model:
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
i.i.d.
\sim P_{\theta_{t}},t\in[-c,c].
It is easy to check that its Fisher
information is

\displaystyle J(t)=n\Bigl\langle\frac{h}{\sqrt{n}},I(\theta_{t})\frac{h}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr\rangle=\langle h,I(\theta_{t})h\rangle.

We will now consider the problem of estimation of function
\varphi(t)=f(\theta_{t}),t\in[-c,c]
based on
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\sim P_{\theta_{t}}

and use van Trees inequality (see
[
37
]
, Theorem 2.13) to obtain a minimax lower bound for this estimation problem.
Let
\pi
be a smooth probability density on
[-1,1]
with
\pi(1)=\pi(-1)=0
and such that
J_{\pi}:=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{(\pi^{\prime}(s))^{2}}{\pi(s)}ds<\infty.

Denote
\pi_{c}(t):=\frac{1}{c}\pi\Bigl(\frac{t}{c}\Bigr),t\in[-c,c].
Then, it follows from van Trees inequality that

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{t\in[-c,c]}{\mathbb{E}}_{t}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\varphi(t))^{2}\geq\frac{(\int_{-c}^{c}\varphi^{\prime}(t)\pi_{c}(t)dt)^{2}}{\int_{-c}^{c}J(t)\pi_{c}(t)dt+\frac{J_{\pi}}{c^{2}}}.

(11.2)

Note that

\varphi^{\prime}(t)=\frac{\langle h,f^{\prime}(\theta_{t})\rangle}{\sqrt{n}}

and

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\langle h,f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta_{0}),f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}.

Next, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}|\varphi^{\prime}(t)-\varphi^{\prime}(0)|=|\langle h,f^{\prime}(\theta_{t})-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle|\leq\|h\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{t})-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\leq\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta).

Therefore,

\displaystyle n\Bigl(\int_{-c}^{c}\varphi^{\prime}(t)\pi_{c}(t)dt\Bigr)^{2}\geq\Bigl(\Bigl(\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})-\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigr)\vee 0\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{4}(\theta_{0})\Bigl(\Bigl(1-\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigr)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\Bigr)\vee 0\Bigr)^{2}\geq\sigma_{f}^{4}(\theta_{0})\Bigl(1-\frac{2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\Bigr).

Also, since
\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{-c}^{c}J(t)\pi_{c}(t)dt=\int_{-c}^{c}\langle h,I(\theta_{t})h\rangle\pi_{c}(t)dt=\langle h,I(\theta_{0})h\rangle+\int_{-c}^{c}\langle h,(I(\theta_{t})-I(\theta_{0}))h\rangle\pi_{c}(t)dt

\displaystyle\leq\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta_{0}),f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle+\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\|h\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})\Bigl(1+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigr).

It then follows from (
11.2
) that

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{t\in[-c,c]}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{t}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\varphi(t))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\geq\frac{1-\frac{2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}}{1+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{J_{\pi}}{c^{2}\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}}.

(11.3)

We will use the following simple lemma.

Lemma 11.1
.

For all
u\in E^{\ast},

\displaystyle\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}u,u\rangle\geq\|I(\theta_{0})\|^{-1}\|u\|^{2}.

proof.

Since
I(\theta_{0})
is a covariance operator, there exists a Euclidean space
{\mathbb{H}},

{\rm dim}({\mathbb{H}})={\rm dim}(E),

and a linear operator
A:E\mapsto{\mathbb{H}}
such that
I(\theta_{0})=A^{\ast}A.
Since
I(\theta_{0})
is invertible, it is easy to check
that operator
A
is also invertible. This implies that its adjoint operator
A^{\ast}:{\mathbb{H}}\mapsto E^{\ast}
is invertible and
(A^{\ast})^{-1}=(A^{-1})^{\ast}.

Moreover, we have
I(\theta_{0})^{-1}=A^{-1}(A^{-1})^{\ast}.
It is also easy to see that
\|I(\theta_{0})\|=\|A\|^{2}=\|A^{\ast}\|^{2}.

Therefore, for all
u\in E^{\ast},

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|A^{\ast}(A^{\ast})^{-1}u\|^{2}\leq\|A^{\ast}\|^{2}\|(A^{\ast})^{-1}u\|^{2}=\|I(\theta_{0})\|\langle(A^{\ast})^{-1}u,(A^{\ast})^{-1}u\rangle

\displaystyle=\|I(\theta_{0})\|\langle A^{-1}(A^{-1})^{\ast}u,u\rangle=\|I(\theta_{0})\|\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}u,u\rangle,

implying the claim.
∎

It follows from the lemma that
\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})\geq\|I(\theta_{0})\|^{-1}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}.
We plug in this lower bound in the right hand side of inequality (
11.3
) to get

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{t\in[-c,c]}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{t}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\varphi(t))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\geq\frac{1-2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}}{1+\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{J_{\pi}\|I(\theta_{0})\|}{c^{2}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}}.

(11.4)

Recall also that, by (
11.1
),

\displaystyle c^{2}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}=\frac{n\delta^{2}}{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}}.

Thus, we can rewrite (
11.4
) as follows:

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{t\in[-c,c]}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{t}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\varphi(t))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\geq\frac{1-2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}}{1+\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{J_{\pi}\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}}{n\delta^{2}}},

(11.5)

which easily implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{t\in[-c,c]}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{t}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\varphi(t))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq 1-D^{\prime}\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}{n\delta^{2}}\Bigr)

(11.6)

with a constant
D^{\prime}\geq 2.
The last bound is trivial when the numerator of the right hand side of (
11.5
) is negative.
Otherwise, the bound is a consequence of the following simple inequality: for all
A\in[0,1]
and
B\geq 0,

\frac{1-A}{1+B}\geq 1-A-2B.

Since
\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta
for all
t\in[-c,c],
it immediately follows from (
11
) that

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f(\theta))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq 1-D^{\prime}\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}{n\delta^{2}}\Bigr).

(11.7)

It remains to replace in bound (
11
)
\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})
with
\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta).
To this end, we
will prove the following lemma.

Lemma 11.2
.

Suppose that
\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\leq 1/2
and
\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}\leq 1.

Then

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\Bigl|\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}-1\Bigr|\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+6\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}.

proof.

Let
B:=I(\theta)-I(\theta_{0}).
Then

I(\theta)^{-1}-I(\theta_{0})^{-1}=I(\theta_{0})^{-1}((I+BI(\theta_{0})^{-1})^{-1}-I),

where
I=I_{E^{\ast}}
is the identity operator in
E^{\ast}.
Under the assumptions that
\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta
and
\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\leq 1/2,

we have
\|BI(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\leq 1/2
and
(I+BI(\theta_{0})^{-1})^{-1}=I-BI(\theta_{0})^{-1}+(BI(\theta_{0})^{-1})^{2}-\dots.
This easily implies
that, for
\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta,

\displaystyle\|I(\theta)^{-1}-I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\leq\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\|BI(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}{1-\|BI(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\|I(\theta)-I(\theta_{0})\|\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta).

We also have

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)-\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})=\langle(I(\theta)^{-1}-I(\theta_{0})^{-1})f^{\prime}(\theta),f^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle+\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta),f^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle-\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta_{0}),f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle.

For
\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta,
the first term in the right hand side of this equality is bounded as follows:

\displaystyle|\langle(I(\theta)^{-1}-I(\theta_{0})^{-1})f^{\prime}(\theta),f^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle|\leq 2\|f^{\prime}(\theta)\|^{2}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigl(\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}+\|f^{\prime}(\theta)-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|(2\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|+\|f^{\prime}(\theta)-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigl(\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}+2\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=2\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigl(1+2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr).

For the second term, the following bound holds:

\displaystyle|\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta),f^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle-\langle I(\theta_{0})^{-1}f^{\prime}(\theta_{0}),f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|(2\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\|f^{\prime}(\theta)-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|+\|f^{\prime}(\theta)-f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|)

\displaystyle\leq\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|(2\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta))

\displaystyle=\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr).

Using these two bounds and the inequality
\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})\geq\|I(\theta_{0})\|^{-1}\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2},
we get, for
\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta,
that

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}-1\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigl(1+2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigl(1+2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(2\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}^{2}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|^{2}}\Bigr).

Since
\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\leq 1/2
and
\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}\leq 1,

we can simplify the bound to get

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}-1\Bigr|\leq 2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+6\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}.

∎

To complete the proof, note that, by (
11
) for all estimators
T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}),

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f(\theta))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}\geq\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f(\theta))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq 1-D^{\prime}\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}{n\delta^{2}}\Bigr).

Since, by Lemma
11.2
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta_{0})}\leq 1+2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|^{2}\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+6\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|},

it follows from the last two bounds that

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta}\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f(\theta))^{2}}{\sigma_{f}^{2}(\theta)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-D^{\prime}\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)+\frac{\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|}{n\delta^{2}}\Bigr)}{1+2\|I(\theta_{0})\|\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\Bigl(6\frac{\omega_{f^{\prime}}(\theta_{0},\delta)}{\|f^{\prime}(\theta_{0})\|}+\|I(\theta_{0})^{-1}\|\omega_{I}(\theta_{0},\delta)\Bigr)},

which easily implies the claim of the theorem.

∎

Next we prove the minimax lower bound of Proposition
3.1
.

proof.

It is enough to prove a lower bound of the order
\frac{\rho^{2}}{n}
and also a lower bound of the order
(\rho^{2}\frac{d}{n})^{s}
in the case
when
\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq 1
and of the order
\asymp 1
when
\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}>1.

The proof of the first bound is rather simple. It is based on the two hypotheses method (see
[
37
]
, Section 2.3) and it is enough to consider functionals
of the form
f(\theta):=\langle\theta^{(1)}-\theta_{0}^{(1)},u^{(1)}\rangle\phi(\theta^{(1)}-\theta_{0}^{(1)})
(for
s\geq 1
), or
f(\theta):=|\langle\theta^{(1)}-\theta_{0}^{(1)},u^{(1)}\rangle|^{s}\phi(\theta^{(1)}-\theta_{0}^{(1)})
(for
s<1
). Here
\phi
is a
C^{\infty}
function on
{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}}
supported in the ball of radius
2

with center
0
and equal to
1
in the ball of radius
1
with the same center;

u^{(1)}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}}
is a unit vector. The two hypotheses in question are
\theta=\theta_{0}
and

\theta=(\theta_{0}^{(1)}+\rho n^{-1/2}u^{(1)},\theta_{0}^{(2)},\dots,\theta_{0}^{(d)}),
so that
f(\theta)=\rho n^{-1/2}
and
f(\theta_{0})=0.

We skip further details of this part of the proof.

The proof of the second bound is more involved.
The first step is to construct a set of “well separated” vectors
\theta_{\omega}\in\Theta
parametrized by points
\omega
of binary
cube
\{-1,1\}^{d}.
Let
v^{(j)}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{j}},j=1,\dots,d
be fixed unit vectors. For
\omega\in\{-1,1\}^{d},

define

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega}:=\Bigl(\theta_{0}^{(1)}+\rho n^{-1/2}\omega^{(1)}v^{(1)},\dots,\theta_{0}^{(d)}+\rho n^{-1/2}\omega^{(d)}v^{(d)}\Bigr).

By the assumptions on
\theta_{0}
and
\rho,

\theta_{\omega}\in\Theta_{n}(\theta_{0},\rho).

In addition,

\displaystyle\|\theta_{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|=\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}},\omega\in\{-1,1\}^{d}

and also that

\displaystyle\|\theta_{\omega}-\theta_{\omega^{\prime}}\|^{2}=\frac{4\rho^{2}}{n}h(\omega,\omega^{\prime}),\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{d},

(11.8)

where
h(\omega,\omega^{\prime}):=\sum_{j=1}^{d}I(\omega^{(j)}\neq(\omega^{\prime})^{(j)})
is the Hamming distance.
Using Varshamov-Gilbert lemma, we can choose a subset
B\subset\{-1,1\}^{d}
of cardinality at least
2^{d/8}

such that

\displaystyle h(\omega,\omega^{\prime})\geq d/8,\ \omega,\omega^{\prime}\in B,\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq\|\theta_{\omega}-\theta_{\omega^{\prime}}\|\leq 2\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}},\ \omega,\omega^{\prime}\in B,\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}.

The second step is to obtain a minimax lower bound on mean squared error of estimation of
\theta_{\omega},\omega\in B.

To this end, note that

\displaystyle K(P_{\theta_{\omega}}\|P_{\theta_{0}})\leq C^{2}\|\theta_{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|^{2},\omega\in\{-1,1\}^{d}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle K(P_{\theta_{\omega}}^{\otimes n}\|P_{\theta_{0}}^{\otimes n})=nK(P_{\theta_{\omega}}\|P_{\theta_{0}})\leq C^{2}\rho^{2}d\leq\alpha d/8\leq\alpha\log{\rm card}(B)

for a small enough numerical constant
\alpha
(provided that
\rho\leq\frac{\alpha}{2\sqrt{2}C}
). It follows from Theorem 2.3 in
[
37
]
that

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{\theta}}\max_{\omega\in B}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta_{\omega}}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta_{\omega}\|^{2}\gtrsim\rho^{2}\frac{d}{n}.

(11.9)

where the infimum is taken over all estimators
\hat{\theta}\in\Theta_{B}:=\{\theta_{\omega}:\omega\in B\},
based on i.i.d. observations
P_{\theta_{\omega}},\omega\in B.

In the third step, we construct “least favorable” smooth functionals
f_{k},k=1,\dots,d
whose values
on parameters
\theta_{\omega}
are hard to estimate and for which the error rate
(\rho^{2}\frac{d}{n})^{s}
is attained.
The construction is based on an approach suggested by Nemirovski (see
[
30
,
31
]
). Let
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{\infty}
function
supported in the interval
[-1,1]
with
\varphi(0)>0
and let
\phi(x):=\varphi(\|x\|^{2}),x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{l},

l=l_{1}+\dots+l_{d}.

Let
\varepsilon:=\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\wedge 1.

Define

\displaystyle f_{k}(\theta):=\sum_{\omega\in B}\omega_{k}\varepsilon^{s}\phi\Bigl(\frac{\theta-\theta_{\omega}}{c\varepsilon}\Bigr),\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{l},k=1,\dots,d.

Recall that
\|\theta_{\omega}-\theta_{\omega^{\prime}}\|\geq\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2}},\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in B,\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}.

If constant
c>0
is small enough (say,
c<0.1
), then each of the functionals
f_{k}
is a sum of “bumps”
\pm\varepsilon^{s}\phi\Bigl(\frac{\theta-\theta_{\omega}}{c\varepsilon}\Bigr)

supported in disjoint balls with centers
\theta_{\omega},\omega\in B
and radius
c\varepsilon,
and, moreover, the distance between any two balls
is at least
\varepsilon/2.
This easily implies that
\|f_{k}\|_{C^{s}}\lesssim 1.
Moreover, by choosing
\varphi(0)
small enough, one can guarantee that

\|f_{k}\|_{C^{s}}\leq 1.
Most importantly,

\displaystyle f_{k}(\theta_{\omega})=\omega_{k}\varepsilon^{s}\varphi(0),\omega\in B,k=1,\dots,d.

This means that the values
of functionals
f_{k},k=1,\dots,d
on a vector
\theta_{\omega},\omega\in B
could be used to recover the binary coding
\omega
of this vector.

In the last, fourth step of the proof, we show how to use estimators
\hat{T}_{j}
of
f_{j}(\theta_{\omega}),\omega\in B
based on i.i.d. observations
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\sim P_{\theta_{\omega}}
to construct an estimator
\hat{\theta}
of
\theta_{\omega}.
Comparing the upper bound on the mean squared error
of this estimator with the minimax lower bound (
11.9
) will allow us to complete the proof. Let
\delta>0
and let
\hat{T}_{j}
be estimators
of
f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})
such that, for all
j=1,\dots,d,

\displaystyle\max_{\omega\in B}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta_{\omega}}(\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega}))^{2}\leq\delta^{2}.

(11.10)

Let
\tilde{\omega}_{j}=\tilde{\omega}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}):={\rm sign}(\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}))
and let
\tilde{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})=\tilde{\omega}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})\varepsilon^{s}\varphi(0).

Since
f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})=\omega_{j}\varepsilon^{s}\varphi(0),
it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{s}\varphi(0)|\tilde{\omega}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-\omega_{j}|=|\tilde{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})|\leq 2|\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})|.

This implies that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2s}\varphi^{2}(0)h(\tilde{\omega},\omega)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl(\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})\Bigr)^{2}.

Let now
\hat{\omega}\in{\rm Argmin}_{\omega\in B}h(\tilde{\omega},\omega)
and define
\hat{\theta}:=\theta_{\hat{\omega}}.

Clearly, we have for all
\omega\in B,

\displaystyle h(\hat{\omega},\omega)\leq h(\tilde{\omega},\omega)+h(\tilde{\omega},\hat{\omega})\leq 2h(\tilde{\omega},\omega).

Using (
11.8
), we get

\displaystyle\|\hat{\theta}-\theta_{\omega}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\rho^{2}}{n}h(\hat{\omega},\omega)\leq\frac{8\rho^{2}}{n}h(\tilde{\omega},\omega)\leq\frac{8\rho^{2}}{n}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2s}\varphi^{2}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl(\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{\varepsilon^{2s-2}\varphi^{2}(0)}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Bigl(\hat{T}_{j}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f_{j}(\theta_{\omega})\Bigr)^{2}.

Recalling bound (
11.10
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{\omega\in B}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta_{\omega}}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta_{\omega}\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\delta^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2s-2}\varphi^{2}(0)}.

Comparing the last bound with the minimax bound (
11.9
), we get that

\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim\frac{4\delta^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2s-2}\varphi^{2}(0)},

or
\delta^{2}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2s}.
If
\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq 1,
this yields the bound

\delta^{2}\gtrsim\Bigl(\rho^{2}\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s},

otherwise we get
\delta^{2}\gtrsim 1.

This implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{C^{s}}\leq 1}\inf_{T_{n}}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{n}(\theta_{0},\rho)}{\mathbb{E}}_{\theta}(T_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-f(\theta))^{2}\gtrsim\rho^{2s}\Bigl(\frac{d}{n}\Bigr)^{s}

when
\rho\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq 1,
and the lower bound is of the order
1
otherwise.

Finally, note that, for

P_{\theta}=P_{\theta^{(1)}}\times\dots\times P_{\theta^{(d)}},
the conditions

K(P_{\theta^{(j)}}\|P_{\theta_{0}^{(j)}})\leq C^{2}\|\theta^{(j)}-\theta_{0}^{(j)}\|^{2},j=1,\dots,d
imply that

\displaystyle K(P_{\theta}\|P_{\theta_{0}})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}K(P_{\theta^{(j)}}\|P_{\theta_{0}^{(j)}})\leq C^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta^{(j)}-\theta_{0}^{(j)}\|^{2}=C^{2}\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|^{2},

and the last claim follows.

∎

Finally, we provide the proof of Proposition
5.1
of Section
5
.

proof.
First note that it will be enough to prove the minimax lower bound with the supremum over the ball
B=B_{\ell_{\infty}}(t_{0},\delta)

instead of set
G
itself.
Note that, for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in{\rm Int}\Theta,

\displaystyle K(P_{\theta}\|P_{\theta^{\prime}})=\psi(\theta^{\prime})-\psi(\theta)-\langle\Psi(\theta),\theta^{\prime}-\theta\rangle\leq\langle\Psi(\theta^{\prime})-\Psi(\theta),\theta^{\prime}-\theta\rangle.

Therefore, for the model
\{P_{\Psi^{-1}(t)}:t\in B\},
we have

\displaystyle K(P_{\Psi^{-1}(t)}\|P_{\Psi^{-1}(t^{\prime})})\leq\langle t^{\prime}-t,\Psi^{-1}(t^{\prime})-\Psi^{-1}(t)\rangle\leq\|\Psi^{-1}\|_{{\rm Lip}(B)}\|t^{\prime}-t\|^{2},\ t^{\prime},t\in B.

Observe now that
(\Psi^{-1})^{\prime}(t)=(\Psi^{\prime}(\Psi^{-1}(t)))^{-1}=\Sigma_{\Psi^{-1}(t)}^{-1}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{-1}\|_{{\rm Lip}(B)}=\sup_{t\in B}\|\Sigma_{\Psi^{-1}(t)}^{-1}\|=\sup_{\theta\in\Psi^{-1}(B)}\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\rm min}^{2}(W)},

and we have

\displaystyle K(P_{\Psi^{-1}(t)}\|P_{\Psi^{-1}(t^{\prime})})\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\rm min}^{2}(W)}\|t^{\prime}-t\|^{2},t^{\prime},t\in B.

Thus, we can use the bound of Proposition
2.2
with
C^{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\rm min}^{2}(W)}
and
\rho^{2}=\gamma^{2}\sigma_{\rm min}^{2}(W)

to complete the proof.
∎
